Concord first-use (fresh-process, no warm-up) request-head cost measurement, r3 remeasurement: v2.1 versus the
reviewed v3 (4c7b894) versus the corrected v3 (1477d3a), and after one product change (2b82432) versus all three
(V3-O07, spec section 28.4; review 3 section 7 and the R3 instructions), recorded verbatim from real runs.

Supersedes nothing: first_use_linux_2026-09-26_r2.txt is kept unchanged. Companions: head_cost_ and
expansion_scale_linux_2026-09-27_r3.txt, future_sizes_linux_2026-09-27_r3_remeasurement.txt; attribution in
ATTRIBUTION.md, section "r3 remeasurement". These are measurements, not a performance acceptance.

WHAT "FIRST USE" MEASURES. The generated contract description is a `static LazyLock` inside each generated
client type's `contract()` (concord-macros emit/client.rs); each endpoint's description is a `static LazyLock`
derived from it. The whole-description cost (building the client's complete static contract description) is
therefore paid ONCE PER GENERATED CLIENT TYPE PER PROCESS, by whichever operation first needs it. It is not paid
per client instance, per request or per warm plan; building more HeadApi clients or planning again does not
rebuild it. The sequence rows show this: after the first plan of HeadApi, the first plans of its other endpoints
cost only that endpoint's own small lazy description. ExecApi is a different generated client type, so its first
execution builds ExecApi's own (smaller) description even in sequence mode.

Host:        Linux 6.18.44-fc-v37 x86_64, Intel(R) Xeon(R) Processor @ 2.80GHz, 2 CPUs, 7 GiB RAM. The r2 records
             name an "@ 2.10GHz" model string on this host name: the underlying CPU differs, so absolute times are
             NOT comparable with the r2 files; only same-session comparisons below are meaningful. The only job on
             the host (no other cargo/rustc process during any timed block); load averages before/after every
             block are in the logs.

  (unchanged since r2 except future_sizes.rs, which is new in r3)

Probe method (head_first_use.rs, unchanged since r2; sha256 65f59778...): `head_first_use <category>` measures,
in one fresh process with no warm-up, the first operation of one category, with everything the category needs
prepared before the measured region (client, inputs, runtime, loopback responder); the region is the operation
and the drop of its result. Categories: client-build, direct-construction, unbound-construction, binding,
plan-show (client.plan(&unbound)), plan-search, plan-protected, plan-limited (rate policy), execution
(runtime.block_on(exec.bounded().into_future()) on ExecApi incl. opening the loopback connection), and sequence
(all of these in this order in ONE fresh process). Allocation count, allocated bytes, peak live bytes and net
allocations are counted with allocation-counter on the current thread (allocation traffic, not resident memory).

Protocol:
  Block A (v2.1, 4c7b894, 1477d3a; worktree at 1477d3a, clean): 20 samples; per sample and category (the nine
    isolated categories and sequence) one fresh process per tree, the order of the three trees rotated
    cyclically per sample: 20 isolated samples per tree and category plus 20 sequence processes per tree;
    600 processes, every one exited 0.
  Block B (v2.1, 4c7b894, 1477d3a, 2b82432; worktree at 2b82432, clean): the same with four trees rotated:
    800 processes, every one exited 0.
  Block C (1477d3a versus 2b82432 only; A/B): 60 samples of plan-show, plan-search, plan-limited and execution,
    the two trees alternating their order per sample: 480 processes, every one exited 0. (An identical earlier
    A/B run of 60 samples of plan-show/plan-limited/execution was made with a script that kept only its summary:
    1477d3a 121.1 / 120.6 / 499.8 us, 2b82432 112.9 / 112.5 / 519.0 us median. It is disclosed, not used.)
  Commands: <scratch>/r3/bin/head_first_use_<tree> <category>   (exit code after every process in the logs)
  Analysis: a scratch script parses the logs; counts/bytes/peak/net are shown as one value when identical in
  every sample (a range lo-hi otherwise); times are median/min/max over the fresh-process samples.

Supporting instruction counts: valgrind --tool=callgrind over `head_first_use_<tree> plan-show` (one fresh
process each, 1477d3a and 2b82432), quoted in the derived section.

Reading the numbers (attribution: ATTRIBUTION.md, "r3 remeasurement"):

- The whole-description cost is once per generated client type per process (see the top of this file). It is
  the first-plan gap: isolated first plans take 126-144 us in v3 versus 28-32 us in v2.1 (4.1x-4.9x; block A
  and B), with +457..+474 allocations and about +67 KB allocated, 122 allocations retained afterwards (peak
  live 25,800 B). In sequence mode, once the HeadApi description exists, the first search/protected/limited
  plans are within +0.1..+1.0 us of v2.1 (1.01x-1.09x).
- 4c7b894 and 1477d3a are identical in every count and byte except the execution rows (-1,776 B: 81,556 ->
  79,780 B, peak live 55,361 -> 53,585 B), and their first-use times agree within the resolution below (plan
  rows -9%..+2%, execution +3.1% / +1.5% in block A, -0.1% / +7.9% in block B). No first-use time effect of the
  layout correction is established.
- First execution: 1.12x-1.20x the v2.1 time (isolated), 1.06x-1.14x (sequence), +68 allocations (ExecApi's own
  lazily built description, 12,901 B, as in r2) and +14,053 B at 1477d3a (r2: +15,829 B; the difference is the
  -1,776 B of the boxed operation future).
- Client build: +4 allocations, +304 B; time equal within noise (0.98x-1.01x). Construction and binding: first
  use equals warm in counts and bytes, times within a microsecond.
- 2b82432 (allocation-free sensitive-name classification): the first plan allocates 35 fewer times (527 -> 492 show,
  556 -> 517 search, 509 -> 474 protected, 525 -> 490 limited; -384 B, search -416 B) and executes about 52,000
  fewer instructions (contract initialization plus plan about 399,000 -> 346,000, -13%). Its first-use TIME change
  is not established: block B +2.6%..+4.3% (20 samples), block C -0.1%..+0.9% on the plan rows (60 samples, logged),
  the earlier summary-only A/B -6.7%. Fresh-process first-plan time on this host is evidently not proportional to
  instructions at this size (cold instruction/data caches and predictors on code that ran never before); why exactly
  was not established (no hardware counters; whole-process minor page faults of a planning versus a non-planning
  category differ by +1 in v2.1 and +4 in every v3 tree, see the page-fault log).
- Resolution: the same executables differ by up to about +/-8% in median between blocks (for example 1477d3a
  isolated execution 476.9 us in block A, 451.7 us in block B, 526.8 us in block C), so first-use time
  differences below about 10% are not attributed.

Record hygiene (R3): trailing spaces at line ends of verbatim tool output (callgrind annotations and
similar) and trailing blank lines were stripped so the file passes `git diff --check`; no other byte changed.
